Document 007: 10月进出口数据点评：AI量价分化延续，美欧经贸格局生变

Source PDF: all report pdf/10月进出口数据点评：AI量价分化延续，美欧经贸格局生变.pdf
Report source: 国信证券
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

事项：
11 月 10日，海关总署发布进出口数据。我国 10 月出口（以美元计价，后同）同比增长 35.2%；进口同比
增长 41.6%，贸易顺差 1052.77亿美元。
评论：
结论：
10月外贸增速维持高位，电子产业链支撑较强，价格因素推升名义增长。出口同比增长 35.2%，较上月小
幅回落，1—10 月累计增长 22.7%；进口同比增长 41.6%，较上月加快 10.6 个百分点，1—10 月累计增长
28.9%。我们测算，集成电路和自动数据处理设备及零部件合计拉动出口增长 13.6个百分点，仍是外贸高
景气度的核心因素。
出口方面，电子产品支撑较强，传统品类表现分化，AI 品类出口增速量价背离值得关注。10 月出口有三
点变化值得注意，第一，电子产业链仍是出口增长的核心支撑，但金额高增主要依靠均价抬升。集成电路、
计算机相关产品出口金额分别增长 137.1%和 114.8%；但集成电路出口数量反而下降 2.5%，隐含均价上涨
143.2%，若后续涨价放缓、同期基数抬升，名义增速或面临回落压力。第二，传统制造出口仍有亮点，但

增长动能有所分化。通用机械、汽车零配件出口分别增长 34.5%和 32.1%，较上月加快；汽车、家电和玩
具则分别增长 37.6%、20.9%和 27.5%，增速有所回落，传统品类的改善尚不均衡。第三，成品油出口同比
保持高增，环比下行，原因在于国内保供扰动短期发运。月初炼厂为补充国内库存暂停部分出口、假期后
恢复安排，导致出口数量环比下降 27.6%。
进口方面，电子与原材料共同支撑高增，价格贡献较大，实物需求表现分化。一方面，电子产品进口保持
高景气，亚洲产业链贸易活跃。集成电路、计算机相关产品进口金额分别增长 78.3%和 261.0%；自韩国、
东盟进口分别增长 110.2%和 42.0%，相关产业链仍是进口增长的重要支撑。另一方面，部分原材料及汽车
进口仍偏弱，需求恢复存在结构性差异。铁矿砂进口金额下降 11.5%，铜矿砂进口数量下降 11.6%；汽车
及零配件进口金额分别下降 38.5%和 41.7%。
往后看，AI 产业链仍将支撑外贸韧性，但价格效应减弱与贸易政策调整，可能推动出口增速回落。此外，

随着中期选举结束、中欧经贸磋商落地，我国与美欧之间的贸易关系将出现变化。中美经贸存在维持阶段
性稳定的空间，科技领域竞争仍可能趋紧。民主党赢得两院后，特朗普推进政策将面临更多国会制约；我
们判断，稳物价、稳经济的诉求可能增加双方维持谈判的动力，但行政端贸易措施仍有反复风险。与此同
时，对华科技限制具有较强的两党共识，先进芯片、半导体设备和 AI 相关出口管制仍可能收紧，电子产
业链将同时面对需求支撑与政策约束。中欧经贸磋商后，混动汽车出口数量约束将限制相关品类增长，对
欧出口面临“政策确定性改善、数量扩张受限”的双重影响。

### Page 2

图1：外贸量值表
资料来源：Wind、国信证券经济研究所整理
 出口：高增延续，AI品类量价背离
10 出口同比保持较高增速。出口同比保持较高增速，但 9 月冲高后的环比回落值得关注。10 月出口同比
增长 35.2%，较上月小幅放缓，1—10 月累计增长 22.7%；当月金额环比下降 7.8%。国庆假期及节前后集
中发运可能影响月度节奏。外需仍处扩张区间，新增订单边际降温。10 月全球制造业 PMI 为 52.6，较 9
月的 53.0回落；中国制造业新出口订单指数由 50.0降至 48.7。外需尚有支撑、增量动能有所放缓。
从商品结构上看，电子产品高增更多体现在金额端，数量出现收缩。集成电路、自动数据处理设备及其零
部件出口金额分别增长 137.1%和 114.8%，但集成电路数量同比下降 2.5%，金额与数量走势背离。汽车、
家电和玩具出口增速分别回落至 37.6%、20.9%和 27.5%；通用机械、汽车零配件则加快至 34.5%和 32.1%，
传统制造出口并非全面走弱。汽车出口数量同比仍增长 33.5%。

从国别结构上看，亚洲贸易联系仍较活跃，欧美市场增速有所分化。对韩国出口同比增长 92.4%，较上月
明显加快；对东盟增长 46.3%，虽有放缓，仍保持较高增速。对美国出口增长 32.9%，较上月回落；对欧
盟增长 24.3%，总体变化较小。国别表现与电子产业链景气具有一致性。
成品油同比仍强，国内保供放大了环比发运波动。10月成品油出口金额同比增长 80.3%，数量增长 14.6%、
隐含均价上涨 57.3%；数量环比则下降 27.6%。这主要是因为炼厂为补充国内库存，月初暂停部分成品油
出口。

 进口：消费品进口偏弱，加工贸易特征明显
电子产业链继续拉动进口。10 月集成电路、自动数据处理设备及其零部件进口金额分别增长 78.3%和
261.0%，体现相关产业链贸易的活跃度。原油进口金额同比增速由 3.3%升至 41.0%。成品油进口金额增长

### Page 4

45.2%。铜进口量价均有支撑，但价格及结构因素占主导。与此同时，铁矿砂、汽车进口金额分别下降 11.5%
和 38.5%。进口结构仍有分化。
进口来源地同样呈现亚洲较强、欧洲偏弱的特征。自韩国、东盟进口分别增长 110.2%和 42.0%，自日本进
口增长 26.5%，均较上月加快；自欧盟进口则下降 4.2%。这一结构与电子产业链贸易较强相呼应，也说明
进口提速具有较明显的区域与行业集中性。

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 13 日
上週重點訊息
◼ TOPIX 啟動大規模改革：日本交易所集團 10 月 7 日公布 TOPIX 調整結果，符合新制納入標
準的成分股將由 9 月底的 1,636 檔降至 986 檔，另有 35 檔股票新納入。現有成分股中，共
有 683 檔未符合流動性及浮動市值等標準，這些股票將自 2026 年 10 月底起逐步降低權
重，並於 2027 年 10 月重新評估，屆時仍未達標者將於 2028 年 7 月底降至零。此次改革有
助於改善 TOPIX 成分股的流動性及投資價值，並促使企業更加重視市場評價。

◼ 巴西總統選舉波索納洛首輪領先魯拉：巴西最高選舉法院 10 月 5 日公布首輪投票結果，參
議員弗拉維奧・波索納洛取得 47.03%的有效票，現任總統魯拉獲得 45.16%，兩人均未取得
過半票數，將於 10 月 25 日進行第二輪決選。若最終由波索納洛當選將相較有利於巴西股市
表現。
◼ IEA 支持加速釋出既有儲油：國際能源署（IEA）10 月 7 日表示，會員國支持加快釋出 3 月
已承諾提供的儲油，並將優先增加柴油供應。截至目前，會員國已釋出約 3.25 億桶儲油；若
剩餘承諾全數執行，可再向市場提供約 1 億桶供應，有助緩解運輸業與農業面臨的燃料成本
壓力。隨各國持續釋出戰略儲備石油，將有利於在短期內壓抑全球油價進一步上行。
市場展望
◼ 標普 500 獲利預估連續上修，資訊科技為主要推升來源：標普 500 企業獲利預估連續兩季上
修，其中資訊科技產業為主要改善來源，反映 AI、雲端運算及資料中心需求仍然強勁。雖然
美國資料中心仍面臨電力與基礎設施瓶頸，但建置規模持續擴大，AI 投資也逐步延伸至記憶

體、網路設備、資安及企業應用等領域。因此，預期科技類股的獲利動能仍具支撐，具 AI 供
應鏈優勢的台股與日股亦可望延續相關成長動能。
◼ 中天期債券可望兼顧息收與利率風險：美國經濟仍具韌性，加上政府與企業發債供給維持高
檔，可能使市場利率維持在較高水準。雖然投資級債信用利差位於相對低檔，但企業獲利、
現金流及償債能力仍具支撐，較高的美國公債基準利率也使其利息收益維持一定水準。短天
期債券面臨利率下降後的再投資風險，長天期債券則較易受到公債供給及利率波動影響，因
此中天期美國公債及投資級債可望在息收與利率風險之間維持較為平衡的表現。

英業達 (2356 TT)  Trading Buy

產能及訂單展望正向，投資建議 Trading Buy 
投資建議 ：我們預估隨零組件的產能逐漸提升，以及商用筆電
換機潮和 AI PC 的需求帶動下， 2027 年營收將可年增 47.9% ，
然而近期市場對 AI 基礎建設投資的疑慮升溫，可能限制英業達
未來營收上行。綜上所述，我們給予英業達 Trading Buy 的投資
建議，目標價 59 元。  
10/2026 營收 799.66 億元， YoY+46.02% ：英業達 10/2026 營收
799.66 億元， MoM-18.93% ，YoY+46.02% ，較我們預期的 849.20
億元差異約 5.8% ，大致符合預期。  
3Q26 營收 2,720.94 億元， QoQ+0.8% ： 3Q26 營收 2,720.94 億
元， QoQ+0.8% ，YoY+54.3% ；營益率 1.61% ；業外淨支出 13.25
億元；歸屬母公司淨利 25.38 億元，稅後 EPS 0.71 。EPS 季減主
因為業外淨支出。  
PC 市場需求疲弱 ：管理層認為 2026 年為此波循環低點，預估
2027 年全球 PC 市場將呈年增持平至微幅增長 ， 因此可期待 2027
年的 Windows 換機潮以及 AI PC 的需求增長帶動 PC 營收增長 。 
預估 4Q26 營收 2,840.78 億元，稅後 EPS 0.80 元：我們認為
4Q26 供應端長短料問題尚未顯著改善，且 PC 市場維持疲弱，
因此預估 4Q26 營收 2,840.78 億元， QoQ+4.4% ；毛利率 4.18% ；
歸屬母公司淨利 28.75 億元， QoQ+13.3% ，稅後 EPS 0.80 元。  
預估 2027 年營收 15,179.62 億元，稅後 EPS 4.90 元：我們預期
2027 年零組件供需失衡狀況將好轉，在既有專案不變下，伺服
器出貨量將可望年增高個位數，且隨伺服器出貨模式逐漸轉為
L11 乃至 L12， 將帶動營收顯著成長 ， 預估2027 年營收 15,179.62
億元， YoY+47.9% ；毛利率 4.09% ，YoY-0.3ppts ；營益率 1.56% ，
YoY-0.1ppts ，毛利率及營益率衰減主要受產品組合改變所致；

2 群益投顧 
  ESG 
環境：公司於 2026 年通過科學基礎減碳目標倡議（ SBTi ）淨零目標審核，
積極推動節能減碳、再生能源使用及資源循環。依公司揭露， 2025 年共執
行 29 項節能方案，節省約 792 萬度電，廢棄物回收再利用率達 80.3% 。此
外，公司持續推動綠色產品設計， 2025 年取得 861 件產品環保及能效認證，
並透過導入回收材料、降低包裝塑膠使用量及研發伺服器液冷技術，降低產
品生命週期之環境衝擊。  
社會：公司重視員工權益、人才培育及職業安全衛生，持續推動多元共融與
友善職場。 2025 年員工平均教育訓練時數達 97.28 小時，員工參與度調查
回收率為 80.9% ，並投入約 1,186 萬元於社會參與活動，透過生態保育、志
工服務及公益計畫履行企業社會責任。  
公司治理：英業達透過董事會及永續發展相關組織推動 ESG 管理，強化誠
信經營、資訊透明及供應鏈風險控管。 2025 年女性主管占比達 31.6% ，供應
商稽核結案率達 100%， 違反誠信經營受裁罰案件為零件 。 此外 ， 公司於2025
年完成 6.25 億美元 ESG 永續連結聯合授信案，將永續績效指標與融資條件
連結，資金主要用於擴充 AI 伺服器產能及海外製造布局，兼顧營運成長與
永續發展。  
公司簡介  
英業達（ 2356 TT ）為全球主要電子產品設計製造服務（ ODM ）廠商之一，
核心業務涵蓋個人電腦及企業級運算設備 。  公司成立於 1975 年，並 於1997
年在台灣證券交易所上市，早期以電子產品代工為主要業務， 1988 年進軍
筆記型電腦市場， 1998 年進一步拓展伺服器領域，逐步建立個人電腦與企
業電腦雙事業布局。  
個人產品事業主要提供筆記型電腦及桌上型電腦之研發設計與製造服務；
企業電腦事業則涵蓋一般伺服器、 AI 伺服器、儲存設備、網路交換器及機
櫃級解決方案，應用於企業資料中心、雲端資料中心及主機代管中心。公司
具備從伺服器主機板至整機櫃之設計與製造能力 ， 並提供 ODM、JDM 及 CM
等多元合作模式，滿足不同客戶的產品開發與生產需求。  
在全球製造布局方面，英業達於台灣、中國、墨西哥及捷克等地設有企業電
腦製造營運據點，具備接單生產（ BTO ）及接單客製化（ CTO ）能力。隨著
雲端運算、 AI 及資料中心基礎建設需求持續發展，公司積極布局 AI 伺服
器、機櫃整合與先進散熱技術，並透過全球產能配置及研發設計能力，強化
產品競爭力與供應鏈韌性。  
10/2026 營收 799.66 億元， YoY+46.02% ：英業達 10/2026 營收 799.66 億元，
MoM-18.93% ， YoY+46.02% ，較我們預期的 849.20 億元差異約 5.8% ，大致
符合預期。營收月減主因為 PC 市場需求仍未見好轉，且伺服器端受供應鏈
長短料問題影響，導致出貨狀況不如預期。  
3Q26 營收 2,720.94 億元 ，QoQ+0.8% ：3Q26 營收 2,720.94 億元 ，QoQ+0.8% ，
YoY+54.3% ；毛利率 4.17% ， QoQ-0.07ppts ，營收季增部分原因為零組件漲
價帶動 ASP 提升，然而受此影響毛利率因此遭稀釋。此外，產品組合的改
變亦使毛利率承壓；營業利益 43.73 億元， QoQ-7.8% ，YoY+42.6% ，營益率

### Page 3

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 11 月 11 日

3 群益投顧 
1.61%；業外淨支出 13.25 億元；歸屬母公司淨利 25.38 億元，稅後 EPS 0.71 ，
QoQ-35.1% 。 EPS 季減主要受業外淨支出所累。  
營運規模擴大及長短料問題，營運成本上升 ：管理層表示 3Q26 利息費用
22.15 億元，較 3Q25 的 14.76 億元大幅增加 7.39 億元。此外，受供應鏈端
長短料問題影響導致出貨遞延，以及零組件漲價造成營運壓力增加， 3Q26
存貨年增 112.3% 至 1,624.59 億元，帶動存貨週轉天數年增 7 天至 46.2 天。
目前大部分存貨為原料及零組件，成品庫存占比極低，長料主要為短期積
壓，目前沒有存貨跌價損失的風險， 4Q26 營運上仍需視上游供給狀況是否
好轉。管理層另表示，目前的資金需求主要透過短期及中長期的銀行額度融
通，儘管 2027 年營運規模提升帶動資金需求顯著增加，但仍有信心以非股
權稀釋的管道進行籌資。  
PC 市場需求疲弱 ：記憶體漲價及 CPU 缺貨等問題導致 2026 年 PC 市場疲
弱。受 1H26 提前拉貨效應及零組件漲價的影響，縱然 3Q26 為傳統旺季，
英業達 PC 出貨量仍季減高個位數。然而據我們了解，英業達的 PC 出貨以
需求彈性較高的商用為主，約佔 PC 總出貨量的 60-70% ，因此在 ASP 提高
及出貨量衰退的影響下營收仍可實現增長。管理層認為 2026 年為此波循環
低點 ， 預估2027 年全球 PC 市場將呈年增持平至微幅增長 ， 因此可期待 2027
年的 Windows 換機潮以及 AI PC 的需求增長帶動 PC 營收增長。  
2027 年智慧裝置展望樂觀 ：目前在智慧裝置的營運重心為無線通訊模組、
AIoT 以及 Edge AI ，其中無線通訊模組部分布局 4G、5G 通訊，並規劃未來
推進高速有線通訊模組領域。 2026 年智慧裝置原物料漲幅極高，然而目前
供應鏈端狀況已初見好轉，預估 2027 年漲幅趨緩，出貨量將呈個位數增長。
AI 資料中心全年展望不變 ：10/2026 美國期中選舉結果引發市場對資料中心
建置的疑慮，然而英業達表示已與客戶確認訂單並未出現調整， CSP 需求仍
強勁，惟 Neocloud 的通用型伺服器需求受其資金排擠效果影響較大，短期
的出貨量可能會有波動。英業達的德州廠主要是供應全美市場，而非僅針對
德州，因此受資料中心相關法案影響較小，並將會持續發展全球布局。  
伺服器營收將顯著成長 ：英業達目前伺服器營收仍以 GPU 及 ASIC 系列為
主。目前 Nvidia 的 Vera Rubin 將以 L11 及 L12 為主， HGX 系列則主要出
L10 和 L11；AMD 系列則以 L6 的形式出貨 ；ASIC 部分則是個別專案而定 ， ，
整體以 L6 或 L10 出貨。管理層預期 4Q26 伺服器出貨量將持平 3Q26 ，成長
性主要還是受長短料問題壓抑，客戶端需求及產能方面都維持樂觀。我們認
為 2027 年英業達 GPU/ASIC 伺服器出貨量將呈高雙位數增長，且將重心由
過往的 L6 逐漸轉往 L10 至 L12 的組裝，成為營收的主要成長動能，但也因
此預期毛利率 /營益率將難以維持 5%/2% 的表現。  
預估 4Q26 營收 2,840.78 億元，稅後 EPS 0.80 元：我們認為 4Q26 供應端長
短料問題尚未顯著改善，且 PC 市場將維持疲弱，因此預估 4Q26 營收
2,840.78 億元， QoQ+4.4% ；毛利率 4.18% ；營益率 1.67% ；歸屬母公司淨利
28.75 億元， QoQ+13.3% ，稅後 EPS 0.80 元。  
預估 2027 年營收 15,179.62 億元，稅後 EPS 4.90 元：我們預期 2027 年記憶
體、 CPU、PCB 及被動元件等零組件供需失衡狀況將好轉，在既有專案不變
下，伺服器出貨量將可望年增高個位數，且隨伺服器出貨模式逐漸轉為 L11
乃至 L12 ，將帶動營收顯著成長，預估 2027 年營收 15,179.62 億元，

YoY+47.9% ；毛利率 4.09% ， YoY-0.3ppts ；營業利益 237.32 億元，營益率

投資建議 ： 2H26 英業達受限於 PC 市場的疲弱及供應鏈端的長短料問題，
營收成長速度放緩。我們預估隨著記憶體、 CPU、PCB 及被動元件等零組件
的產能逐漸提升，以及在商用筆電換機潮和 AI PC 的需求帶動下，此一情景
可望在 2027 年逐漸緩解。此外，英業達已向客戶確認伺服器展望維持正向，
既有訂單將不會有變動， CSP 的需求仍舊強勁。惟近期市場對 CSP 巨頭的
現金流擔憂上升，未來 Neocloud 及 Enterprise 的需求不確定性較高，且 AI
資料中心的建置亦可能受未來相關法案所限而推遲，可能限制英業達營收
上行。綜上所述，我們給予英業達 Trading Buy 的投資建議，目標價 59 元。

8 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

Key Data  _____________________________________

GS Forecast  __________________________________

GS Factor Profile  ______________________________

Co-Tech’s 3Q26 core business (operating income) was 7/5% lower 
than GSe/BBG consensus, mainly due to higher summer electricity 
rates which led to a 0.7ppt QoQ decrease in 3Q26 GPM, and came in 
1.4/1.5ppt below GSe/BBG consensus. Product mix continues to 
improve with HVLP3+ copper contribution at close to 20% (vs. 
15-20% in 2Q26). Co-Tech’s OPEX increased to NT$90mn (vs. 
NT$83mn in 2Q26). NI was 8/4% lower than GSe/BBG consensus 
mainly due to the lower than expected GM.  
For 4Q26, we expect Co-Tech’s revenue will increase by 8% QoQ, 
mainly driven by (1) price hikes of ~10% QoQ across all copper foil 
products (we saw price hike starting to accelerate from late June, 
and we expect to see prices increase to further accelerate in 4Q26), 
(2) initial shipment of higher ASP/margin next gen products, 
including HVLP5 and RG313+, and (3) higher HVLP3+ copper foil 
contribution due to an increase in capacity (HVLP3+ capacity could 
reach 250 tons/month by end of 2026 vs. 200-250 tons/month in 
3Q26). On GM, we expect the 4Q26 level to be 31.4% (4.7ppt QoQ 
increase), mainly due to the improved product mix while the 
summer electricity rates impact comes to an end (the impact ended 
by mid-October). 
CB issuance plan announced to fund further capacity expansion 
in Malaysia: Co-Tech announced on Nov 6 its plan to issue CB 
(Convertible Bond) of up to NT$7bn, supporting the company’s 
Plant 4 expansion in Malaysia, which the company is expected to 
spend NT$7-10bn on two phases, with each phase expected to add 
~800tons/month of effective HVLP4+ capacity (based on yield of 
50-60%). The two phases are targeted to reach MP by end of 
2028/29, respectively. Full conversion of the CB would result in less 
than 10% EPS dilution per GSe (based on the average share price 
over the past week), while each phase of the expansion could 
contribute NT$11bn+ in revenue at full operation based on our 
estimate (vs. Co-Tech’s 2025 revenue of NT$7.9bn).

### Page 3

Investment view - Capacity expansion extended into 2029, still insufficient to meet 
with client demand 
We maintain our positive view on Co-Tech’s revenue and profitability outlook, 
underpinned by (1) a substantial capacity ramp-up, with Co-Tech’s high-end capacity 
will increase by 3.5x+/75%+/50%+ by 2027/28/29 end (vs. 2026 end), outpacing the 
expansion expected from other major competitors and supporting further market share 
gains, and (2) supply condition for high-end copper foil remains tight, supporting a 
better pricing environment, and we expect price increase to accelerate in coming 
quarters/years. 
We continue to believe Co-Tech is well-positioned to benefit from the structural demand 
for high end copper foil in a shortage environment, as the company has announced 
capacity expansion plans for a new Plant 4 in Malaysia (see here), with capacity already 
secured by multiple customers. The company now targets its monthly HVLP3+ effective 
capacity to reach 833/1666/2500 tons/month by 2027/28/29 end (vs. current HVLP3+ 
effective capacity of 200-250 tons/month). Despite the proactive capacity expansion, 
the planned capacity additions remain insufficient to fully meet with demand from key 
customers. This should enable the company to further expand market share amid high 
end copper foil supply shortage environment, and we expect the company’s HVLP3+ 
market share to further expand to 41/53% in 2027/28E (vs. 19% in 2026E).  
We expect high-end copper foil TAM to reach US$2.7bn by 2028E (Exhibit 1, with 133% 
2025-28E CAGR), following our update on global AI PCB/CCL TAM (see here). We now 
believe the shortage ratio for HVLP3+ industry to be 39-48% in 2026-28E (Exhibit 4, 
slightly lower than our previous estimate, which was 45-55% in 2026-28E, as we factor 
in the latest capacity expansion plans), with the shortage already starting in 2H26, as the 
ramp-up of mainstream AI server projects increasingly adopts HVLP4+ copper foil. The 
wide shortage ratio would suggest an elevated industry UTR and provide further support 
for price increase over the coming quarters/years.  
We maintain Buy on Co-Tech, with a new TP of NT$990 from NT$940.

demand (133% 2025-28E CAGR), which could account for

2.1/2.2/1.2ppt as we factor in a higher contribution from RG copper foil (with GM at 
30%+) based on the company’s latest capacity expansion plans as well as assuming 
higher copper cost, which will lead to slower GM expansion.

22x 2028E EPS, which is +1SD above the company’s upcycle valuation in the past 
10-years, and factoring in our earnings revisions.

Investment Thesis & PT methodology and risks

Investment Thesis - Co-Tech 
Co-Tech is a global leading general server copper foil supplier, with ~60% market share 
in 2025 per GSe. We expect the company to enjoy solid share gain opportunities in the 
near term, considering its proactive HVLP capacity expansion plan and strong 
know-how in the advanced copper foil industry. Also, adoption of HVLP4 copper foil 
from AI servers and 1.6T networking switches starting in 2H26 should be a key demand 
driver for Co-Tech, in our view. Additionally, the company’s proactive pricing strategy 
and ability to improve production yield should drive revenue momentum in the long 
term.  
We are Buy rated as we believe Co-Tech will see better revenue/profitability in the long 
term given its shift to a more premium product mix along with proactive capacity plans 
(Plant 3 to be ready by end of 2027 and Plant 4 phase 1 & 2 to be ready by end of 
2028/29) and this should lead to strengthened valuations.

Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$990 is based on a 22x 2028E P/E, which is 
+1SD above the company’s average upcycle valuation in the past 10-years. 
Key downside risks: (1) earlier than expected CPO adoption in the computing IC, (2) 
slower than expected yield rate improvement speed, and (3) much stronger than 
expected capacity growth from HVLP peers, limiting pricing power

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

### Page 1

Simplo Technology (6121.TWO)
An Established Battery Franchise with Room for a Better Mix
CITI'S TAKE 
3Q26 EPS missed our/BBG estimates by 35%/30% each as notebook pull-
ins faded and low yields on a major US customer’s new smartphone held 
back shipments. We expect higher PC component costs to keep IT demand 
subdued in 1Q27E, while rising contribution from AES could still help margin 
expansion in 2027E. We lower 2026-2028E EPS by 17-20% and revise our 
TP to NT$400 based on 12x 2027E EPS, lowered from 13x reflecting weaker 
visibility on IT recovery. We continue to favor Simplo’s leading market share 
in notebook/smartphone battery franchises and its exposure to AIDC BBU 
growth through AES. 
3Q earnings -17% y/y missed our/BBG estimates by 35%/30% on weaker IT 
shipments — 3Q26 sales of NT$19.9bn fell 2% y/y and 5% q/q, 13%/10% below 
our/BBG forecasts. Notebook customers pulled orders into 1H26 amid rising 
memory prices, while lower yields on a major US customer’s new smartphone 
constrained 3Q shipment. Group GPM rose 0.7ppt y/y to 16.3% on a larger AES 
contribution. However, standalone GPM dropped 1.6ppt y/y 50 9.2% on higher input 
costs and lower volumes. Group OPM rose 0.4ppt y/y to 9.7%, lower than our (12.2%) 
and BBG (11.9%) estimates. Higher taxes also weighed on EPS. 
2027E outlook stays muted for IT — The mgmt. expects IT demand to remain 
lukewarm in 1Q27E as higher component costs put upward pressure on PC prices 
likely weighing on demand. Standalone sales are expected to be flat to slightly down 
y/y in 2027E, with the group sales flat to slightly up on AES’s BBU growth. Mgmt. 
notes the battery-pack inventories at notebook brands are lean at just a few weeks 
and easing cobalt prices could bring down ASP in 2027E. A new non-IT product line 
is growing severalfold from a small base, and the mgmt. expects it to make up >5% 
in 2028E with a GPM above group’s average. 
Implications — We cut 2026-2028E earnings estimates 17-20% for 2026-2028E 
reflecting 3Q miss and a slower IT recovery, resulting in a 10-18% cut on our sales 
forecasts. We lower our TP to NT$400 based on a target P/E of 12x from 13x on 2027E 
EPS, reflecting the component inflation likely delaying the timing of IT recovery. We 
stay Buy on Simplo’s established notebook and smartphone battery franchises and 
AES’s exposure to growing datacenter BBU demand. AI PC adoption could lift 
battery-pack ASPs over time, and an estimated 6% dividend yield should help 
cushion downside.

Simplo Technology (6121.TWO)
09 November 2026 Citi Research
5
Simplo Technology
Company description
Established in 1992, Simplo specializes in designing and producing battery
packs for Notebooks, tablet PC, and electronic vehicle products. With strong
execution and cost advantages, Simplo continues to broaden its client base.
Its major customers include PC OEMs (such as HPQ, Dell, Asus, Acer, and
Apple) and NB ODMs (such as Quanta, Compal, Wistron, and Inventec).

Investment strategy
We rate Simplo shares as Buy. Simplo’s heating fundamentals are driven by
the following reasons. 1) Japan supplier's strategy change on focusing EV
battery pack and de-prioritizing NB which provides Simplo more orders wins;
and 2)  Non-IT: according to our industry survey, Simplo has been qualified by
global ISPs for the battery back units (BBU) for their data centers.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$400 is based on a target P/E of 12x 2027E EPS, on par

with its historical average since 2010.
Risks
Downside risks that may cause the shares to continue to trade below our
target price include: 1) weaker than expected end product sales; 2) intensified
competition; and 3) worse-than-expected overall NB/tablet/PC growth.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Date: 2026-11-09

### Page 1

Taiwan Consumer Discretionary

What’s new: We attended PMX’s investor conference after the company 
released its 3Q26 results on 9 November 2026. We reaffirm our Buy (1) call 
as we see better-than-expected demand for its semiconductor, AI server 
and medical segments.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results missed mainly on FX loss. PMX’s 
3Q26 revenue of TWD2,417m (+5.9% QoQ, +24.6% YoY) beat our 
estimate by 8.5% and was in line with the Bloomberg consensus. Its gross 
margin came in at 23.9% vs. 24.6% in 2Q26 and 21.7% in 3Q25, missing 
our estimate (24.2%) and the consensus (25.1%), mainly due to some one-
off costs ramping up at the new Vietnam plant. With higher-than-expected 
opex, operating profit of TWD340m (+10.0% QoQ, +54.4% YoY) missed 
our and the consensus estimates by 1.3% and 6.0%, respectively. With an 
FX loss of TWD23m, its net profit of TWD271m (+13.3% QoQ, +0.1% YoY) 
missed our and consensus estimates by 11.1% and 10.2%, respectively. 
EPS was TWD2.36 in 3Q26. PMX’s 3Q26 revenue breakdown is as 
follows: auto sales at 49% (+13% YoY), semiconductor, AI server & other 
sales at 20% (+66% YoY), storage device sales at 13% (+25% YoY), 
medical sales at 10% (+13% YoY), and sports sales at 8% (+42% YoY). 
PMX also posted October 2026 monthly sales of TWD816m (+3.3% MoM 
and +33.8% YoY; +22.0% YoY for 10M26), which accounted for 35.6% and 
31.3%, respectively, of our previous and the consensus forecasts of 4Q26 
revenue.

Segment transformation continues with upbeat guidance for 4Q26 
and 2027. For 2026, PMX maintained its overall 20-25% YoY revenue 
growth guidance with semiconductor, AI server & others segment growing 
by at least 100% YoY. For 2027, management expects revenue to grow by 
20%+ YoY with the semiconductor, AI server & others segment to grow by 
100%+ YoY; medical, by 50%+ YoY; storage device, by 10% YoY; auto, flat; 
and sports to decline by 5%. We revised down our 2026-28E EPS by 0.3-
8.5% after factoring in its 3Q26 results.

What we recommend: We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating with an unchanged 
12M TP of TWD205, now based on a target PER of 17x (from 18x; above 
the 15x average of its past-3-year range of 11-25x) on our 1-year forward 
EPS forecast. We lower the PER target slightly on its decelerating earnings 
momentum. Key downside risks: lower than-expected global auto, medical, 
and AI server component demand; worse-than-expected gross margin.

How we differ: Our 2026-27E EPS are 18-22% below the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely as we are more conservative about PMX’s revenue and 
operating margins.

9 November 2026

3Q26 results review: weaker-than-expected margins

➢ 3Q26 net profit missed our and consensus estimates by 10-11% 
➢ Semiconductor, AI server and Others segments to support growth  
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) call with unchanged 12M TP of TWD205

Company profile 
Founded in 1987, Global PMX provides mechanical parts for automotive, medical equipment, and 
storage device components. Its major automotive clients include Continental, Bosch, BorgWarner, ZF, 
Hilite and Tesla. Other major clients are Seagate, Medtronic, and Zimmer.

### Page 5

5

Global PMX (4551 TT): 9 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 8 May 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
The CEO and Chairperson of PMX are not the same individual. The board includes 3
independent directors, including 1 female independent director. The Chairman has held this
position at PMX for more than 20 years, indicating the company's strong management capability
and leadership, in our view.
Capital management 1
PMX's payout ratio was 80-90% before 2018, but dropped to 29-69% in 2019-25, which we
attribute to its acquisitions over the past few of years (eg, merger with Sixxon in 2020). We view
these acquisitions as reasonable capital management as PMX’s product line expansion has led
to lower risk exposure due to diversification, helping PMX's 2020-24 revenue to stay relatively
intact.
Related party &
transaction 2 No major revenue from related-party transactions in 2020-25, and we observed no severe issues.
S Product quality & safety 2
PMX holds numerous international product quality certifications, including ISO9002, QS9000 and
ISO/TS16949, which help to regulate the production phases, from development to quality control
and sales. Besides, products made by PMX are 100% compliant with laws/regulations and
market expectations on health and safety, so we believe that product quality and safety concerns
are not a high risk for PMX.
S Materials sourcing & efficiency 2 Global PMX's supply conditions of raw materials (mainly copper and steel) are stable, and its suppliers are required to sign the company’s Supplier Commitment Statement. Therefore, we believe materials sourcing and efficiency will be under control going forward.
S Product design & lifecycle management 2 PMX has adopted strategies that avoid price competition by targeting production of higher value- added products (including auto, medical, semiconductor, and green energy products). Thus, we believe that PMX is continually improving its product lifecycle management.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 1

Kinik

Strong advanced nodes and packaging 
expansion support decent earnings

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 1,050 TWD | Price: 737.00 TWD

3Q26 margin beat on favorable product mix  
Kinik’s 3Q26 sales grew 8% QoQ, mainly driven by solid diamond disk demand from N2 
and advanced packaging capacity ramp and increasing shipments of high-end 
test/reclaim wafer, more than offsetting weakness at Thailand ABU subsidiary. 3Q26 
GMs improved by 220bps QoQ to 42.2% on improving product mix across ABU and DBU 
segments, taking 3Q26 EPS to NT$3.91(vs. BofAe/Street NT$4.09/NT$4.08).  
4Q26 sales grow on continued advanced nodes expansion 
The company guided 4Q26 sales to grow slightly QoQ (BofAe + 2% QoQ), implying full-
year revenue growth of +25% YoY, backed by strength across diamond disk, test wafer, 
and reclaim wafer amid ongoing advanced nodes capacity build-out. Strong contribution 
from high-margin DBU segment, coupled with a favorable mix shift in ABU driven by 
ABF diamond wheels, should lift 4Q26 GMs further to 43.5%, in our view.  
Solid DBU demand and SBU ramp underpin '27-28  
On accelerated 3nm, 2nm and A16 capacity ramp at full utilization, alongside benign 
mature node supply/demand landscape and steady engagement with Intel on 18A/14A, 
we forecast Kinik’s diamond disk shipment to +48% CAGR over '26-28. Meanwhile, 
Kinik’s SBU sales could deliver +22% CAGR over '26-28 as 10% price hike on selected 
products take effect from early 2027 and carrier wafer demand from Micron HBM grow 
more meaningfully from 2H27. Beyond its existing business, grinding wheels for 
advanced packaging are currently under customer qualification, and would provide 
upside potential with TAM much larger than diamond disk.  
Diverse drivers and margin expansion warrant re-rating 
We keep our 2026/27/28 EPS largely unchanged at NT$14.98/26.23/36.84. Reiterate our 
Buy rating and PO of NT$1,050, still based on 32x 2H27-1H28E P/E (vs. its historical 
range 9-42x), supported by robust sales/earnings CAGR of +32%/+57% over '26-28.

Company Sector 
Semiconductor Capital Equipment

Company Description 
Kinik was established in 1953 as a conventional grinding 
wheels supplier. Since 1990-2000s, the company has 
transformed itself into a semiconductor consumables 
supplier including CMP pad conditioners (diamond disks) 
and reclaim/test wafers.

Investment Rationale 
We have a Buy rating on Kinik. We acknowledge its 
technology for CMP diamond disk is solid to support its 
gain in TSMC advanced nodes and Intel along with growing 
reclaimed and testing wafer demand. We expect to see 
margins expansion from mix upgrade in coming years.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
Kinik (KIKCF) 
Our Price Objective of NT$1,050 is based on 32x 2H27-1H28E P/E, which is at upper 
half of the company's historical trading range of 9-42x. In our view, the valuation 
multiple is underpinned by 
1) around +57% earnings CAGR in 2026-28E supported by +32% sales CAGR growth and 
GMs/OpMs expansion, 2) ROE expansion to around 30% in 2025-28E, 4) net cash of 
around NT$30/share, and 5) free cash flow generation of around NT$50/share per 
annum across 2025-27E.

Downside risks to our estimates and expectations are 
1) weaker market demand, especially in the cloud AI investment moderation, leading to 
lower utilization across its major foundry and memory customers and undermining the 
company's revenue growth and operating leverage 
2) slower manufacturing technology migration by its foundry and memory customers, 
dragging the product upgrade cycle for Kinik and potentially spurring more competition 
from its peers. 
3) failure to execute or delay of technology roadmap, potentially leading to an order shift 
to its competitors.

Addressing investor questions
about EMC's share gain for
switch board CCL
Additional supply enquiries point to a potential revenue opportunity for EMC.
Our supply chain checks indicate that PCB makers supplying NVIDIA (covered by
Joseph Moore) have approached EMC (2383.TW, OW) for additional Copper Clad
Laminates (CCL) supply support for VR200 switch boards (32-layer PCBs). If these
enquiries translate into incremental orders, we estimate the resulting revenue

contribution would represent a single-digit percentage of EMC’s total revenue.
We expect EMC to retain a majority share of CCL supply for VR200 switch
boards. Our positive view reflects EMC’s technological capabilities and our
preference for market share leaders. While supplier diversification can strengthen
long-term supply security, we believe the ability to sustain yields and deliver
consistent product quality over time remains a key consideration in supplier

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

### Page 1

Wistron

Reiterate Buy: 3Q26 beat, better margins; 
AI momentum intact; cut PO to NT$250

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 250.00 TWD | Price: 174.50 TWD

3Q26 beat: solid margins on favorable product/client mix 
Wistron’s 3Q26 GM/OPM of 5.90%/4.24% came in above BofAe of 5.36%/3.66% and 
consensus of 5.55%/3.70%. We attribute the strong margins to greater sales scale 
(+28% QoQ, +102% YoY; 1%/8% above BofAe/consensus), more favorable product/client 
mix in AI business, and better results from Wiwynn, mainly supported by general/ASIC 
server momentum (see also Wiwynn 3Q26 result note). Thus, OP was 17%/24% above 
BofAe/consensus. With non-operating loss of NT$6.2bn (including net interest expense 
and FX loss), NP was 16%/27% above BofAe/consensus (Exhibit 3).  
AI order momentum intact; ongoing project ramp-ups 
We see no change in Wistron’s AI order momentum into 4Q26 and 2027-28. We expect 
4Q26/1Q27 sales to rise 11%/8% QoQ, driven by GPU/CPU rack project ramp-ups, with 
rising ASP and spec upgrade. We also see potential sales upside from more new 
client/project wins in 2H27-2028. Although the GM decline trend is inevitable for AI 
server ODMs, we expect Wistron to post solid core OPM (i.e. excluding Wiwynn) into 
2027-28, with rising operating efficiency and more diversified client/project base. On 10 
Nov, the company announced additional capex plans of subtotal around NT$12.8bn for 
adding facilities and equipment in Taiwan, mainly to support new AI-related businesses, 
and another US$17.8mn for expanding server manufacturing capacity in Mexico.  
Trim PO to NT$250 with a lower P/E set-up; reiterate Buy  
We lift our 2026-28E EPS by 7-21% (but 2027-28E EPS is still 8-9% below consensus) 
to reflect the 3Q26 results beat, solid AI momentum, and sustained core OPM. Yet, we 
lower our PO to NT$250 (12x 4Q26-3Q27E EPS) from NT$265 (15x 2H26-1H27E EPS), 
with the valuation base rollover and a lower P/E multiple. We now apply 12x (1SD above 
historical average since 2022; Exhibit 8), vs. prior 15x (1.5SD above) on valuation de-
rating across AI supply chain amid macro uncertainty. Nevertheless, we reiterate Buy on 
Wistron in view of its solid AI sales momentum with a broadening client/project base.

Key Metrics

Company Sector 
IT Hardware

Company Description 
Wistron is one of the top-5 ODMs globally. Server/storage 
and PC business accounted for 73% and 24% of sales in 
2025, respectively. Key server/storage customers include 
Dell, Lenovo, HPE, Cisco, Nvidia, AMD, Intel. Key NB 
customers include Dell, Lenovo, Acer. Wistron was spun off 
from Acer (2353 TT) in 2000 and was incorporated in May 
2001. It went publicly listed on the Taiwan stock exchange 
in Aug 2003. It is headquartered in Taipei, Taiwan.

Investment Rationale 
We assign a Buy rating to Wistron in view of improving 
sales/profit mix, solid outlook from Wiwynn, high exposure 
to server business after exiting from low-margin iPhone 
assembly business, and improving core OPM. We believe 
Wistron will benefit from Dell's strong demand in Nvidia AI 
GPU/CPU rack as the key supplier.

### Page 7

Price objective basis & risk 
Wistron (WICOF) 
Our PO of NT$250 is based on 12x 4Q26-3Q27E EPS. We use 12x valuation multiple 
(around 1SD above historical P/E average since 2022) in view of high server mix, a key 
position in AI server supply chain with exposure both to component/rack business, and 
ongoing core OPM improvement.

Upside risks to our PO are (1) stronger server market demand, especially for AI, (2) 
stronger PC demand, (3) share/order win in AI server business, and (4) favorable FX 
trend.

Downside risks to our PO are (1) softer AI server demand due to AI GPU short supply or 
supply chain hiccup, (2) weaker PC market demand amid inflation/macro uncertainties, 
(3) GM pressure due to higher labor/logistics cost, (4) more intensified competition in 
NB and server business, and (5) unfavorable FX movement (USD/RMB and/or USD/NTD) 
to weigh on GM and/or non-operating items.

iQmethod SMis the set of BofA Global Research standard measures that serve to maintain global consistency under three broad headings: Business Performance, Quality of Earnings, and validations. The key features of 
iQmethod are: A consistently structured, detailed, and transparent methodology. Guidelines to maximize the effectiveness of the comparative valuation process, and to identify some common pitfalls. 
iQdatabase ®  is our real-time global research database that is sourced directly from our equity analysts’ earnings models and includes forecasted as well as historical data for income statements, balance sheets, and cash

### Page 1

M
Update
Compal Electronics  | Asia Pacific
Server Ramp Takes Shape but
Valuation Keeps Us UW
Compal’s server expansion is progressing, with revenue
contribution rising to ~12% in 3Q and management targeting 30-
40% in CY27. However, weaker PC volumes and diminishing
scale benefits continue to pressure margins. At ~14x CY27E P/E,
valuation remains stretched.
3Q OpM missed. See our  3Q26 Results Summary                   .
PC business facing demand and profitability headwinds: Compal expects a mid-

teens q/q decline in 4Q26 shipments because of higher selling prices. This weakness
should persist into CY27; the company projects a likely double-digit y/y unit decline,
though it admitted that visibility remains low. However, rising ASPs from mix
changes and cost pass-through could keep 4Q PC revenue up sequentially.
Servers have become a key growth driver: Servers rose from ~5% of the mix in 1Q
to ~12% in 3Q, with a CY27 revenue contribution target of 30-40%. While 4Q26

server revenue is guided to be flat q/q, Compal is aggressively scaling its capacity. It
now primarily ships from Taiwan, and a major L10/11 ramp will begin in 2Q27 at its
new Texas plant. It currently does L10 HGX for Neocloud customers and L6 for a US
enterprise customer; it will do L10/11 for enterprise customer(s) as well, and is
actively approaching CSP customers for ASIC L11 solutions as well.
Margins to remain pressured: Overall margins were pressured by the PC segment,

driving a sequential decline in 3Q owing to lower PC volumes and utilization. This
margin headwind should intensify in 4Q because of shrinking PC business scale.
Conversely, server gross margin should remain resilient in the high single digits to
10%,  staying flat q/q.
4Q preview: We estimate that 4Q revenue will grow ~8% q/q, to NT$263bn (+38%
y/y), primarily driven by higher PC ASPs despite declining volumes. GM should
contracting ~10bps q/q, to 4.3% (-150bps y/y), with declining PC profitability, mainly

owing to diminishing scale benefits. This leads to our OP estimate of NT$2.8bn
(+25% q/q, +6% y/y) and NP estimate of NT$1.7bn (-34% q/q, +21% y/y), for EPS of
NT$0.39. Our 4Q net profit estimate is down q/q because of lower non-op income.
Stay UW with price target unchanged at NT$23.0 (10.4x CY27 P/E). We think
valuation looks quite stretched now at ~14x CY27e P/E. We prefer other ODMs like
Wistron (6x), Hon Hai (12x), Wiwynn (8x), or Quanta (9x).

Morgan Stanley appreciates your support in
the 2027 Extel Asia Research Survey.

3Q26 Results Summary
3Q26 gross margin miss:
• Revenue was NT$243bn (+2% q/q, +30% y/y), and GM was 4.4% (-30bps q/q, -
130bps y/y), 80bps and 40bps below our estimate and the Street, respectively,
driven by lower PC margins.
• As a result, OP was NT$2.bn (-21% q/q, -16% y/y), 33% below our estimate/34%
be;low the Street, for OpM of 0.9% (-30bps q/q, -50bps y/y).
• Non-op profit was higher than expected, so net profit reached Nt$2.6bn (-18% q/q,

+33% y/y), 15% above our estimate/2% above the Street, for EPS of NT$0.59.

4
Price Target and Valuation Methodology
Our 12-month price target is unchanged at  NT$23.0: Our price target is our base case
scenario value, derived from a multistage residual income (RI) model. We use the
company's beginning equity plus the present value of all expected residual income in
excess of the cost of capital. The RI is positive when ROAE is above the cost of capital and
negative when it is below.
We assume a cost of equity of 9.1%  (beta of 1.3, equity risk premium of 6.0% and risk-free

rate of 1.5%), a medium-term growth rate of 5%, and a terminal growth rate of 3% (all
unchanged).
Our new price target implies a 2026e P/E of 10.4x and a 2027e P/E of 8.5x, below the five-
year average of 13.8x. We view these as fair, because we factor in more conservative
assumptions for its consumer business and a weaker margin outlook for Compal. The
company's venture into the "new businesses" (e.g., auto, server, etc.) is constraining its
margin profile as it tries to shift from 80%+ PC to more commercial exposure.

Our bull and bear case values are also unchanged, at NT$38.0 and NT$17.0,
respectively.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Hon Hai Precision (2317.TW)
Base case scenario value, derived from a residual income valuation model. Our key assump-
tions include a cost of equity of 8.5%, a medium-term growth rate of 13%, and a terminal
growth rate of 3%.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected iPhone sell-through
n Faster progress in AI server business
n Faster EV business development progress
n Any new M&A activity that could improve sentiment

Risks to Downside
n Lower iPhone sell-through
n Smaller profit contribution from AI business
n Slower EV business development progress
n Geopolitical developments that could negatively affect foreign investment
Quanta Computer Inc. (2382.TW)
Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions include a cost of equity of 9.0% (beta of
1.2, equity premium of 6.0% and risk-free rate of 1.5%), a 7.0% medium-term growth rate, and
a 3% terminal growth rate.

Risks to Upside
n Stronger-than-expected NB demand
n Stronger-than-expected Apple Watch demand
n Stronger-than-expected server demand
n Faster-than-expected AI server penetration
Risks to Downside
n Weaker-than-expected NB demand
n Softer-than-expected Apple Watch demand
n Weak margin performance owing to rising labor costs and sales shortfalls
n Fierce price competition in the mega data center segment
n Slower-than-expected AI server penetration

Wistron Corporation (3231.TW)
Base case, residual income valuation. Key assumptions: 8.7% cost of equity, 5.0% medium-
term growth rate and 3% terminal growth rate.
Risks to Upside
n Faster-than-expected divestiture of consumer electronics business
n Stronger-than-expected NB demand
n Margin expansion from better product mix

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Document 288: [402340] SK스퀘어(402340) SK하이닉스 최대주주SK스퀘어(402340) SK하이닉스 최대주주SK하이닉스 최대주주 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[402340] SK스퀘어(402340) SK하이닉스 최대주주SK스퀘어(402340) SK하이닉스 최대주주SK하이닉스 최대주주 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

SK 스퀘어 (402340/KS) 
SK 하이닉스 최대주주 
SK 하이닉스 4 분기도 배당 주당 20,000 원 이상  
SK 하이닉스는 전일 주당 20,000원(실적반영: 10,000원, 키옥시아 특별배당: 
10,000원)의 3분기 배당을 발표하였다. 키옥시아 특별배당은 3분기 배당과 26년 회
계연도 결산배당으로 나누어 지급예정이다. 이에 SK스퀘어는 2조 9,220억원(1분기
와 2분기 배당수입은 각 548억원)의 배당수입을 수취하게 된다. SK하이닉스는 26년 
전체 현금 배당 규모는 30조원 이상을 발표했는데, 3분기까지 지급하는 배당금은 14.6
조원이기 때문에 4분기에도 최소한 주당 20.000원 이상의 배당이 예상된다. 이 경우 
SK 스퀘어의 26 년 배당수입은 6 조원에 달할 것으로 예상한다. SK 스퀘어는 ‘26년
~’28년 경상배당수입의 30% 이상을 주주에게 환원하는 주주환원정책을 보유하고 있
어 SK스퀘어의 현 시가총액 대비 27년 주주환원율은 최소 1.5% 이상 될 전망이다.

SK 하이닉스 자사주 취득·소각으로 높아질 지배력 
SK하이닉스는 20조원의 자사주 매입을 발표하였다. 이 중 10조원은 임직원 보상목적
이며, 10조원은 주주환원 목적이다. 26년 총 60조원 이상의 자사주 취득·소각 예정(8
월 결의분 40 조원 포함)으로 임직원 보상 목적의 자사주 매입은 제외되기 때문에 26
년 실적 확정 후 추가적으로 10조원 이상의 자사주 취득이 예상된다. 기 취득한 자사주 
40조원(3.3%)는 11월 5일 소각 예정으로 소각 이후 SK스퀘어의 SK하이닉스 지분
율은 20.5%(2 분기말 기준)에서 21.2%로 상승하게 된다. 이번 발표한 10 조원
(1.0%) 자사주 소각시 SK 스퀘어의 지분율은 21.4%이 되며, 추가로 10 조원을 추가 
소각한다면 21.7%까지 지분율이 상승하게 된다.

SK 하이닉스 대비 높은 베타 
SK스퀘어에 대한 투자의견 매수를 유지한다. SK스퀘어 NAV의 98%가량을 차지하
는 SK하이닉스의 현금배당 증가로 SK스퀘어의 주주환원 재원이 확대되고, SK하이닉
스의 자사주 취득·소각으로 지배력이 강화되기 때문이다. SK하이닉스는 전일 컨퍼런스 
콜을 통해 29년까지 D램과 HBM 물량이 사실상 모두 솔드아웃된 상황이라 밝히며 주
가 반등 가능성이 높은데, SK 스퀘어 베타(1.8)는 SK 하이닉스(1.7) 대비 높기 때문에 
SK 하이닉스 주가 반등에 따른 SK 스퀘어의 동반 주가 상승을 기대할 수 있다. 현재 
SK스퀘어의 NAV 대비 할인율은 46.5%이며, SK하이닉스가 자사주60조원 소각시 
할인율은 49.4%까지 상승하게 된다.

2026-10-29

지주회사/통신서비스/ESG. 최관순 / ks1.choi@sks.co.kr / 3773-8812

본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다. 
종목별 투자의견은 다음과 같습니다.

Document 305: An RBA peak of greater conviction (2026-11-13)

Source PDF: all report pdf/An RBA peak of greater conviction (2026-11-13).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #rba
An RBA peak of greater conviction
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
08:56 November 13 2026 Share
We already believed the RBA cash rate was likely at a peak, even if RBA officials were less
convinced. A deeper housing downturn and early signs of cracks in the consumer sector
point to an even softer 2027. Fading inflation risks from oil also boost our conviction on
rates being at a peak.

### Page 2

It was already our view that the RBA was likely done hiking the cash rate after the November
increase. Since then, our conviction on that call has strengthened. Later today we will publish
the November Market Outlook with revised forecasts. GDP growth, the labour market and housing
market have all been revised down in 2027. Underlying inflation has been revised down slightly in the
second half of 2027, bringing the year-ended figure for calendar 2027 more decisively inside the

RBA’s 2–3% target range than our previous forecast round. The unemployment rate forecast has
been revised up to peak above 5%. Based on this revised forecast profile, an increase in the cash
rate from here would be unlikely and – we would argue – undesirable.
A range of considerations underlie this forecast revision. The housing market has undershot our
forecasts over the past couple of months. Turnover and financing activity have been coming in
under our earlier expectations. Mortgage loan inquiries have taken another leg down in October after

the September rate hike, after finding something of a level in prior months, with the additional impact
of the November rate hike still to come.
We are also seeing early signs of a turn in the consumer. Consumer income and spending have
so far been quite resilient to pressures from fuel prices and rising interest rates. But our Westpac
Card Tracker is starting to show that discretionary services spending is turning down again in Q4,
joining the discretionary goods category that was already quite weak. We would not over-emphasise

this point, but it does imply that the risks of further surprising resilience are fading.
Subsequent to the November Monetary Policy Board (MPB) meeting, business survey data
has shown weaker conditions and a sharp decline in forward orders. They also show further
evidence that current inflation is mostly a non-labour cost shock that is not being fully
passed through to consumer prices.
As we noted following the November MPB meeting, oil and fuel prices are already materially

lower than the track assumed in the RBA’s forecasts. Dated Brent is nearly one-fourth lower than
on 28 October, the date the RBA’s forecasts were finalised, and spot Brent is around 15% lower. This
significantly lowers the risk of a new wave of pass-through of fuel costs to other prices, as well as
easing consumer inflation perceptions and expectations.
In the media conference following the November meeting, Governor Bullock flagged that it was a

good time to “take stock” of the policy tightening to date. Both we and the market read this as a
signal that the MPB would pause at the December meeting. The 6:3 split vote at the meeting also
suggests that some external members are more attentive to downside risks than the internal
members.
A rate hike in February 2027 cannot be ruled out but it is looking even less likely given signs
in the data and surveys. The MPB will at least discuss a hike in February. The staff and senior

officials remain sensitive to inflation risks and have a downbeat view of supply capacity growth.
Given what we are seeing in the economy now, though, a rate hike in 2027 would require
either a material new inflation shock that has not yet occurred, or would constitute a policy
error on the part of the RBA (something that we will never forecast as a base case, knowing the
talents and capabilities of the staff and the Board). We concur with the dissenting voters in the MPB

that the downside risks need more attention. Policy is clearly restrictive, and the risk of overdoing the
inflation correction is growing. And by the time we get to February, we think there will be more
evidence that the domestic economy is slowing materially, and the inflation outlook with it.

### Page 3

内容提要 
2026 年 10 月份，CPI 同比增长 0.7%，较前月下降 0.2 个百分点，供给充足食品价格继
续下行，居民消费价格整体运行平稳，震荡前行。10 月能源价格继续上涨，对当前 CPI 增
速形成主要的拉升因素。 
2026 年 10 月份，PPI 同比增长 5.0%，较前月上涨 1.2 个百分点，上游原材料价格继续
上涨，尤其能源价格的大幅上涨，推动工业品价格增速继续上行。一方面，8 月以来的美伊
战争加剧，国际能源价格再次上涨；另一方面，促消费叠加整治“反内卷”式竞争，耐用消
费品价格增速明显上涨。 
10 月通信工具价格同比增长 16.1%，较9 月上涨 1.8 个百分点， 对本月CPI 增速形成拉
升因素；交通工具用能源价格同比增长 20.2%，较 9 月上涨 5.3 个百分点，亦对 CPI 增速形
成主要的拉升因素。与外贸进出口、PPI、工业企业利润等指标相契合，通信工具价格持续
上涨， 价格增速高位运行， 主要受科技大爆发， 终端需求旺盛推动的价格不断上涨， 是经济
结构调整的阶段性结果。

图 1 CPI、PPI 当月同比增速（%） 图 2 CPI、PPI 环比增速（%） 
数据来源：Wind，北京大学国民经济研究中心 数据来源： Wind，北京大学国民经济研究中心

需求推动，通信工具价格继续上行 
2026 年 10 月份，CPI 同比增长 0.7%，较前月下降 0.2 个百分点，供给充足食品价格继
续下行，居民消费价格整体运行平稳，震荡前行。10 月能源价格继续上涨，对当前 CPI 增
速形成主要的拉升因素。

### Page 3

北京大学国民经济研究中心 
本月食品价格同比下降 2.9%，降幅较前月下降 1.2 个百分点，是导致本月 CPI 增速小
幅下滑的主要因素。10 月果蔬供给充足， 鲜果、 鲜菜价格同比下降3.8%、16.9%， 降幅分别
较 9 月扩大 2.3、8.8 个百分点，是拉低本月 CPI 增速的主要因素之一。 
10 月通信工具价格同比增长 16.1%，较9 月上涨 1.8 个百分点， 对本月CPI 增速形成拉
升因素；交通工具用能源价格同比增长 20.2%，较 9 月上涨 5.3 个百分点，亦对 CPI 增速形
成主要的拉升因素。与外贸进出口、PPI、工业企业利润等指标相契合，通信工具价格持续
上涨， 价格增速高位运行， 主要受科技大爆发， 终端需求旺盛推动的价格不断上涨， 是经济
结构调整的阶段性结果。

图 3 CPI 食品与非食品当月同比增速（%） 图 4 CPI 食品与非食品环比增速（%） 
数据来源：Wind，北京大学国民经济研究中心 数据来源：Wind，北京大学国民经济研究中心 
食品项中， 从同比看， 食品价格增长-2.9%，较前月下降 1.2 个百分点，供给充足使得食
品价格同比继续负增长。总体而言， 食品项八大类价格同比分化 （1 涨、7 跌、0 持平 ），其
中鲜菜价格同比幅度最大（-16.9%）；较上月同比变动分化（3 正、4 负、1 不变），其中鲜
菜价格同比变动最显著（-8.8%）。

能源价格波动，PPI 增速上行 
2026 年 10 月份，PPI 同比增长 5.0%，较前月上涨 1.2 个百分点，上游原材料价格继续
上涨，尤其能源价格的大幅上涨，推动工业品价格增速继续上行。一方面，8 月以来的美伊
战争加剧，国际能源价格再次上涨；另一方面，促消费叠加整治“反内卷”式竞争，耐用消
费品价格增速明显上涨。 
8 月以来的美伊战争加剧，使得国际原油价格上涨，10 月 PPI 中，石油和天然气开采
业、石油煤炭及其他燃料加工业价格分别同比增长35.0%、29.9%，较 9 月上涨 10.5、8.3 个
百分点， 本月能源及相关工业品价格增速继续明显上涨，是推动本月 PPI 增速上涨的主要因
素。

图 13 生产资料、生活资料价格同比增速（%） 图 14 生产资料、生活资料价格环比增速（%） 
数据来源：Wind，北京大学国民经济研究中心 数据来源：Wind，北京大学国民经济研究中心 
从同比看，10 月 PPI 增长 5.7%，较前月上涨 0.7 个百分点。上游原材料价格上涨，尤
其是原油价格上涨，是导致本月 PPI 增速上行的主要因素。生产资料价格同比增长 7.4%，

北京大学国民经济研究中心 
北京大学国民经济研究中心简介： 
北京大学国民经济研究中心成立于 2004 年，挂靠在北京大学经济学院。依托北京大学，重点研
究领域包括中国经济波动和经济增长、宏观调控理论与实践、经济学理论、中国经济改革实践、转轨
经济理论和实践前沿课题、政治经济学、西方经济学教学研究等。同时，本中心密切跟踪宏观经济与
政策的重大变化， 将短期波动和长期增长纳入一个综合性的理论研究框架， 以独特的观察视角去解读，
把握宏观趋势、剖析数据变化、理解政策初衷、预判政策效果。 
中心的研究取得了显著的成果， 对中国的宏观经济政策产生了较大影响。 其中最具有代表性的成
果有： （1）推动了中国人口政策的调整。中心主任苏剑教授从 2006 年开始就呼吁中国应该立即彻底
放弃计划生育政策， 并转而鼓励生育。 （2） 关于宏观调控体系的研究： 中心提出了包括市场化改革、
供给管理和需求管理政策的三维宏观调控体系。 （3） 关于宏观调控力度的研究：2017 年 7 月， 本中
心指出中国的宏观调控应该严防用力过猛， 这一建议得到了国务院主要领导的批示， 也与三个月后十
九大报告中提出的“宏观调控有度”的观点完全一致。（4）关于中国经济目标增速的成果。2013 年，
刘伟、 苏剑经过缜密分析和测算， 认为中国每年只要有6.5%的经济增速就可以确保就业。 此后不久，
这一增速就成为中国政府经济增长速度的基准目标。 最近几年中国经济的实践也证明了他们的这一测
算结果的精确性。 （5）供给侧研究。刘伟和苏剑教授是国内最早研究供给侧的学者，他们在 2007 年
就开始在 《经济研究》 等杂志上发表关于供给管理的学术论文。 （6） 新常态研究。 刘伟和苏剑合作的
论文“新常态下的中国宏观调控”（ 《经济科学》2014 年第 4 期） 是研究中国经济新常态的第一篇学术
论文。 苏剑和林卫斌还研究了发达国家的新常态。 （7） 刘伟和苏剑主编的 《寻求突破的中国经济》 被
译成英文、韩文、俄文、日文、印地文 5 种文字出版。（8）北京地铁补贴机制研究。2008 年，本课
题组受北京市财政局委托设计了北京市地铁运营的补贴机制。该机制从 2009 年 1 月 1 日开始被使
用，直到现在。 
中心出版物有： （1） 《原富》杂志。 《原富》是一个月度电子刊物，由北京大学国民经济研究中
心主办，目的是以最及时、最专业、 最全面的方式呈现本月国内外主要宏观经济大事并对重点事件进
行专业解读。 （2） 《中国经济增长报告》 （年度报告） 。该报告主要分析中国经济运行中存在的中长
期问题，从 2003 年开始已经连续出版 14 期，是相关年度报告中连续出版年限最长的一本， 被教育部
列入其年度报告资助计划。（3）系列宏观经济运行分析和预测报告。本中心定期发布关于中国宏观
经济运行的系列分析和预测报告，尤其是本中心的预测报告在预测精度上在全国处于领先地位。

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.41 / 9 November 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
In brief. The week was dominated by large market swings triggered by
the US midterms results. In the end, energy prices end the week much
lower, bond yields somewhat lower and stock prices down in tech-heavy
markets and slightly up elsewhere, notably in Europe. Incoming data
confirmed a picture of resilient growth overall. The Bank of England

hiked its policy rate by 25 bps and signaled it could do more. Market
pressure on France eased somewhat.
EUROZONE
Energy pushes inflation up, but underlying pressures remain rela -
tively contained. Headline inflation rose to 4.0% y/y in October, its
highest in three years, strengthening the case for another ECB rate
increase in December. Energy remained the main driver, while core in-
flation rose by 2.6%. The ECB wage tracker points to negotiated wage

growth slowing to 2.3% by September 2027. Meanwhile, retail sales fell
0.7% m/m in September and the average interest rate on new business
loans increased 13bp to 3.90%.
To watch next week: September trade balance, Q3 GDP second esti -
mate and employment (Friday).
France
EVs boost activity, market pressure eases somewhat. After already
bolstering household consumption in Q3, new car registrations rose
7% y/y in October (+4% y/y YTD), driven by another surge in electric

car sales (+81% y/y in October; +78% y/y YTD). In parallel, manufac -
turing output rebounded by 0.5% m/m in September, supported by
the automotive sector (+7.5% m/m but -5.5% y/y) and capital goods
(+2.6% m/m and +4.8% y/y). However, industrial production fell by
0.5% m/m (and 1.3% y/y), as a result of a decline in electricity produc-
tion (-4.2% m/m). The composite October PMI was revised down by
1.8 points from the flash estimate to 46.6 (-4.5 m/m), dragged down by

services. This sharp downgrade likely reflects the hit to sentiment from
the October 17 riots; however, precedents suggest activity itself should
not be visibly impacted. Appropriately indifferent to the meandering
journey of the draft budget through Parliament, the OAT-bund spread
narrowed to 155 bps (-10 bps in one week) amid a broad-based decline
in government bond yields across advanced economies; Thursday’s
long-term debt auction, the last of the year, was well-subscribed. Stri-

ving to meet its deficit target of 5.4% of GDP in 2026, the government
announced EUR3.8bn of spending cuts to offset EUR3.7bn in additional
expenditure. The High Council for Public Finance nevertheless sees the
deficit reaching 5.5%. The 2027 budget will start being discussed by
the Senate from next week, in its original version, after the national
Assembly unanimously rejected its amended version that would have
led to a 5.9% deficit.
To watch next week: Q3 unemployment rate and the Banque de France

monthly business survey (Tuesday).
Germany
Industrial activity picks up. New orders rebounded 4.4% m/m in Sep -
tember and industrial production increased 0.7%, with the auto sec -
tor delivering particularly striking gains of 10.5% in orders and 17.1%
in production. Mechanical-engineering orders also recovered (+8.3%),
while pharmaceutical production increased by 8.0%. By contrast, large
orders in aerospace, shipbuilding, rail and defence fell by 10.4%, while

machinery and equipment production declined by 6.5%. October's com-
posite PMI was revised up to 53.4, signaling continued strong expan -
sion.
Italy
Services keep the economy above water as manufacturing slips back.
The composite PMI eased to 50.8 in October, its lowest since May.
Manufacturing returned to contraction at 49.4, its weakest reading
since January, while services strengthened to 52.0 and recorded a fifth
consecutive month of growth. Both sectors reported increasing input-

cost and output-price inflation.
UNITED KINGDOM
The BoE hikes and leaves the door open to more. The Bank of England
raised Bank Rate by 25bp to 4%, its first increase since August 2023, in a
more hawkish than expected 8-1 vote, with Catherine Mann favouring
a 50bp hike. The November Monetary Policy Report sees CPI inflation
peaking around 5% y/y by mid-2027 and upgraded Q4 2026 growth to
2.2%, while cutting the 2027 and 2028 growth projections. The com -

posite PMI meanwhile improved to 52.9, its highest since February,
with new business growing at its strongest pace in nine months and
private-sector employment increasing for the first time in 25 months.
To watch next week: Q3 GDP , business investment, industrial produc-
tion (Thursday).
UNITED STATES
Post election market jitters and a hot jobs report. The Democrats’
unexpectedly sweeping victory in the midterms (the largest wave
since 1974) initially led markets to rally on (i) relief that the President

recognised the results, and (ii) hopes of a swift end to the Iran war
(oil prices and bond yields down, stock prices up). But this was soon
followed by a sharp equity sell-off on 5 November, with the S&P 500
down 4.4% and the Nasdaq down 5.0%, erasing USD2.7 Trillion of mar-
ket capitalization in one day. Why? Because of the realization that vo -
ters strongly supported AI-restrictive measures on the ballot (46 out
of 47) and candidates — for Congress and Governor seats — in favour

of curtailing data centers buildout. By the week-end, however, senti -
ment had turned around again, helped in part by a much stronger than
expected jobs report: payrolls increased by 165,000 in October against
a 70,000 consensus, while the previous two months were revised hi -
gher and unemployment edged down to 4.1%. Average hourly earnings
rose 0.4% m/m, their strongest monthly pace since November 2025.
Manufacturing activity also strengthened, with the ISM rising to 55.5,

and the non-manufacturing index easing to a still buoyant 54.5.
To watch next week: October CPI (Tuesday); October PPI (Friday).
JAPAN
Wage growth accelerates. Scheduled contractual earnings increased
3.7% y/y in September and total wage growth accelerated to 4.6%,
helped by strong bonus payments. Real wage growth remained posi -
tive at 2.2% y/y.
EMERGING ECONOMIES
Portfolio outflows. According to IIF data, non-resident portfolio invest-
ment flows recorded USD33.8bn of outflows in October, the largest

monthly outflow since March and up from USD19.3bn in September.
Equities accounted for USD31.7bn, led by USD28.3bn from emerging
Asia, as doubts over AI-investment returns and profit-taking hit North
Asian markets particularly hard. Taiwan and South Korea suffered a
further sharp market correction in the first week of November.

### Page 2

7
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.41 / 9 November 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
ASIA
PMI: expanding except for the Philippines. The ASEAN PMI rose to 53.1
in October from 52.1, pointing to faster expansion. India remained ro -
bust despite a slight decline (54.7). In Taiwan and South Korea, the
manufacturing PMIs declined but remained expansionary, at 54.9 and
51.3, respectively. Indonesia’s real GDP growth accelerated to 5.5% y/y

in Q3 2026 from 5.3% in Q2 marking its fastest pace since 2013.
China
The PBOC steps in as equity correction deepens. The central bank in -
creased its use of liquidity tools designed to support equity purchases,
including the special relending facility for share buybacks and stake in-
creases and a third round of the Securities, Funds and Insurance Com-
panies Swap Facility, for which 31 institutions applied for more than
RMB100bn. The measures follow heavy stock-market losses between

June and October. The PBOC also raised the reserve-requirement ratio
on foreign-currency deposits to 6% from 4%, effective 15 November, in
an effort to slow the renminbi's appreciation.
Weak domestic inflation pressure: In October, CPI inflation slowed to
0.7% y/y from 0.9% in September. Fuel prices increased by 20.2% y/y,
food prices fell -2.9% and core inflation slowed to 0.8% y/y from 1.0%.
Producer price inflation continued to rise (reaching 5.7% vs. 5.0% in

September), principally driven by higher energy and electronics prices.
To watch next week: External-trade data (Tuesday).
EMEA
Central Europe
PMIs expansionary except in Romania. Poland’s PMI hit the 50-mark
for the first time since April 2025 thanks to export orders. PMI was
also higher in Czech Rep. (55), supported by new orders, output and
employment. Hungary’s PMI fell marginally to 53.1.
Poland and Czech Republic central banks hold and hike respectively,

as expected.
Gulf
PMIs resilient except Qatar (deteriorating further to 46.3).
Saudi Arabia: Massive budget deficit in sight. Saudi Arabia recorded a
budget deficit of USD22.5bn in Q3, narrowing by 5% y/y. But over the
first nine months of 2026, budget deficit reached USD65.1bn, up from
USD48bn in 2025, despite a rather good revenue performance (+2.8%
total; +7.8% for non-oil revenues; -1.3% for oil revenues). The 8% rise in
spending in Q1-Q3 partly due to the war will make it difficult to meet

the 2026 revised target (deficit of 4.9% of GDP).
LATIN AMERICA
PMIs remain weak across the region, with only Colombia in expansion.
Brazil’s central bank pauses rate cuts, whereas Mexico’s central bank
cuts rates whilst signalling an upcoming pause. On 4 November, Bra -
zil’s central bank held its Selic rate at 13.75%, interrupting a series of
five consecutive cuts, amid rising inflation (4.6% y/y in September, up
from 4.2%), a sharp post-election real depreciation on fiscal concerns,

and higher energy prices. The central bank also revised up its 2027
inflation forecast to 4.7% (from 3.9%) and removed forward guidance
hinting at further cuts. Mexico’s central bank unexpectedly cut its po -
licy rate by 25bp to 6.25% on 5 November. This decision came despite
above-target inflation (3.5% against a 3% target), a peso depreciation
of more than 9% against USD since September, and relatively strong Q3
GDP growth (3.2% y/y). The central bank signaled that rates would now

remain on hold for an extended period.
COMMODITIES
Geopolitical relief delivers a dramatic energy-price reversal. Mar -
kets have priced in the reduced risk of short-term conflict escalation
through a sharp decline in prices. Beyond this, however, they lack di -
rection, wavering between positive signals (the resumption of indirect
negotiations between the belligerents) and less favorable ones (in -
creased vessel damage in the strait). Brent dropped 7.6% over the week

to USD96.8/bbl as markets priced a reduced risk of near-term conflict
escalation. European gas prices fell almost in lockstep, declining 7.7%
to EUR87.6/MWh. US commercial crude inventories continued to de -
cline, although more modestly than over the preceding four weeks.
Diesel crack spread’s reaction to US elections has been much more
limited than for crude. EU gas storage ended the refilling season at 75%
of capacity (vs. 80% goal).
y/y = year-on-year

m/m = month-on-month
bp = basis point
pp = percentage point
mb = million barrels
MBtu = Million British Thermal Units

Document 411: Open mouth operations (2026-11-13)

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #rba
Open mouth operations
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
13:34 October 23 2026 Share
The RBA does not want to promise what it will do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks.
Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for every nuance. Lately, there seems to have
been more RBA communication to pore over. The method of delivery has also evolved. This has

### Page 2

Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks for clues to future rate moves. They will
have plenty to pore over in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry of fireside chats
this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday.
Next Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before Senate Estimates, just as the
quarterly CPI is released. (This timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After the

decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a media release and press conference. More
speeches will follow, including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just following the RBA Review but also more
recently. There may be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication outside that certainly
feels more frequent than a couple of years ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little

sparse outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary appearances. We have counted
58 public engagements so far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website. This compares
with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in 2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events were related to economics, markets
and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed as well. There are fewer set-piece

speeches on policy and a lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few years ago, there
are more interviews across a wide range of media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It
was a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the 7:30 Report; it is hard to imagine him
agreeing to an Assistant Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general audience. Central banks want to explain

why they do what they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising but the household
sector is not the source of the inflation.
Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief
Communication Officer highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting media conference is
designed to speak to the public. Market participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
sometimes caused confusion when tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different tone and

varying complexity.
The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly using speeches directed to professional
audiences to discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such as neutral rates and
financial conditions. They are also highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more ways
than just squeezing mortgage holders.
One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises

about the future path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running commentary about how it
is interpreting recent data. These interpretations are themselves useful signals about what the RBA
thinks it might do.

### Page 3

the primary audience. So if you think that what the Governor said in the media conference sounded
more ‘dovish’ than what another official says in a more market-oriented forum, it is partly because she
is trying to convey that the RBA understands that its recent policy actions are making things harder for
many Australians. But this is in much the same way that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It
does, but that will not stop the treatment.

A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted material such as the minutes and set-
piece speeches than in unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be careful with their
words in unscripted settings. If the tone differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have reduced the pressure to make the set-piece

speeches more accessible. Research on central bank communication often uses measures such as
the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was
beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison suggests four speeches from 2026 (1, 2, 3,
4) were harder to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches scored in the 11 to 12
range, which is high-school-level complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and 15, which is

academic or technically dense text requiring university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-Governor Phil Lowe also scored better
on these metrics than the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that readability metrics do
not necessarily imply better clarity if the concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has

begun to put more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those invisible ‘star’ variables
(NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences
has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on the full range of transmission channels from
interest rates to inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour supply channel). This is

understandable in a world of supply shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt they would be comforted, though, by the
Deputy Governor’s explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the smallest of the
transmission channels from interest rates to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on inflation. It raises the question of whether

other policy tools or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on inflation through the other
channels without hitting a minority of households disproportionately.
We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases to summarise the position. From ‘narrow
path’ to ‘not ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once entrenched, a phrase can
outlast its usefulness.

### Page 4

Related articles
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record the presentations to the Board and the
members’ discussion. The minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-meeting
statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The
August 2024 minutes were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how sensitive the forecasts
were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed

Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s communication have not changed. It
wants to explain – and perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how it interprets
incoming data, especially inflation, the labour market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business of providing ‘forward guidance’ about
future moves. Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular future path for interest

rates. Some academic literature argues that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high, for
that matter) independently amplifies the transmission of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-
period interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield curve. Central banks including the RBA
have certainly tried jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view, though, is that the
potential cost of reputational loss if things do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a

slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides the signal about future policy directions. This
is why it was noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour market as ‘a little tight’ after the
September meeting, even though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We have been
noting for some time that the RBA staff have a downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the

RBA’s oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not
surprise us.

Document 458: US SECTORS CALL_ Information Technology, Industrials & Utilities

Source PDF: all report pdf/US SECTORS CALL_ Information Technology, Industrials & Utilities.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-08
